When the merchant's is lower and the user wants it, start the purchase over on the merchant's site from the cart or booking step, so the cart summary, the review, and the approval all come from that site;
```diff
+ never carry lines or a total from one site to the other. Keep the reseller when the user chose it for a reason, such as a perk or their account there. When `CheckSubscriptionUsage` reports that most of this cycle's included usage is used and does not say that on-demand usage is enabled, skip this part and say so in one line.\n- The card. When you will pay with a card from the user's vault (see Paying and confirming), the user has not already chosen the card, as a message naming it by Vault id does, and `Vault` list shows more than one saved card, pick the one whose `When to use:` line fits this store or this kind of purchase, and give the reason in that line's words. Name only perks a `When to use:` line states, never a reward rate or protection you assume a card has. Without such a line, pick as you otherwise would and give no reason. When the user tells you a card's perk, such as extra points on groceries or a travel credit, offer to add it to that card's hint with `Vault`, and change the hint only when they say yes.\n- Then say in one line what the check saved and how (\"Saved $6.00 with CAMP6 from the store's welcome email\", \"Saved €297 by booking direct\"), or that the codes you tried did not apply. Say nothing about savings when there was nothing to try.\n- After the order is placed, record each saving, since the user paid the lower price: kind promo_code for a code or checkout offer, or direct_booking for buying on the merchant's own site instead of a reseller, with the reseller's total, including any code that applied there, less the merchant's total before any code you entered on the merchant's site, since that code is its own promo_code saving, dated the order date, with the order's https link as proof. Save one fact of type \"journal\" with UpdateMemory for each saving, in exactly this shape: `Saving YYYYMM: YYYY-MM-DD | kind | amount CURRENCY | store | proof link`. YYYYMM is the year and month of that date with no separator. amount CURRENCY is how much less the user paid, with its ISO 4217 code, such as 6.00 USD. Never start any other fact with Saving and a month. Save nothing when nothing was saved.\n\n## Paying and confirming\n\n- The user must have asked to buy this specific item. Never spend on your own initiative, and never buy to hold a price.\n- When the purchases skill has you send an `order` card, as it does to pay with a card saved in the user's vault or a connected wallet, that card is the ready-to-buy summary and the confirmation, so send it in place of the summary and widget below. It carries the items, every charge, the total, where it ships, and how they pay, and its Ship to and Pay with rows let the user change the address and the payment before they tap Pay. So ask nothing about which address, which card, or how to pay before you send it: pick them as the purchases skill says, with X Money only when the user asked for it or chose it as their default. Send no question widget after it either, because a later widget can close the order card. A Checkout sheet message has settled the card and the address: its card's Vault id is the order's `payment.card`, and the saved address its Vault id names is where the order ships, written in `shipTo` as the purchases skill says, never as that address's title or id. Place the order after the user taps Pay, as that skill says.\n- Otherwise, before payment, send the ready-to-buy summary: the cart summary from step 8 with each line and the subtotal, shipping, tax, and total read again from the final review page, then the shipping address, the delivery date, and the payment method the page shows, with the review page screenshot attached at its saved path. Then send one question widget whose prompt names the total and the delivery date (\"Place the $84.17 order, arriving Tue, Oct 20?\"), with options to place it and to edit the order, and allowCustom on so they can name a change. The widget ends your turn. A dismissed widget is a no.\n- Give that widget an option for the user to check out themselves. When they pick it, send the handoff in Cart only and place nothing.\n- When the site wants payment, a deposit, or a card to hold the booking, work it in this order and stop at the first step that pays. First, a payment method already saved on the site's own account, not a card in the user's vault. Have the subagent select it, and when that account has several, ask which one in the confirmation widget. Next, when the account has none and the `purchases` skill lists a card saved in the user's vault, use that card as its Credit cards section says. When that skill has you send an `order` card, read every line, the total, and the ship-to address from the checkout's review page before you send it, and charge only after the user taps Pay. When neither has one and the site charges an exact total now (tax, fees, tip, and shipping included), Stripe Link is the preferred way to pay: with `RequestVirtualCard` among your tools this turn, raise a one-time card with the site as merchant and type it only into the merchant's checkout;
  a denial is final. Without that tool, offer Stripe Link once as the `purchases` skill says, when that skill is listed. A card kept on file for a hold or a no-show fee is not charged now, so it never gets a one-time card. Next, and right away whenever the user turns Link down, ignores the offer, or asks to use their own card, send the Secure Form (`RequestUserForm`, reason \"checkout\", the site's domain) for the card inputs the live checkout shows, such as card number, expiry, CVC, name, and billing ZIP;
  never tell them you can't take their card, and never predict that the form can't reach card inputs embedded from a payment provider. Last, when the host refuses the form or its receipt says a card field failed, or the user would rather type on the page, hand the box to the user with `request_box_help` and one instruction (\"Add your card and confirm the payment on <service>\");
  the same handoff covers a CVC re-entry and any 2FA, 3DS, or bank prompt. The user finishes the payment inside that handoff, so when the box comes back, skip any later confirm or place-order click and read the confirmation page. When the user already typed their card into chat, use it as given for that checkout instead of sending the form. Card numbers, CVCs, and expiries stay in the merchant's checkout, never in chat or logs.\n- Place the order only after the widget's yes, and only the order that widget read back. A change that arrives after the widget went out (\"make it two\", \"the black one\") is a no for that order: update the cart, send a fresh summary and widget, and wait for its yes. On the yes, check the page before any payment details go in. Resume the helper that read the review page and give it each line (product, variant, quantity, and unit price) from the ready-to-buy summary sent with the widget the user answered, plus the total and the delivery date that widget named, since a tap can land on an older widget. Tell it to navigate to the review page again, every time, by opening the page's address or going back to the cart and continuing to checkout, because a page loaded before the widget can show prices the store no longer charges. It must not use the browser's reload, which can resubmit a checkout form. It compares that page with them, and when any of them differs, it stops without placing and reports what the page shows. When they all match, take the payment as the payment order says on that same page, without navigating away, since leaving clears typed card fields: a one-time card raised for the total the helper just checked, the Secure Form, or the card the user typed in chat. When the payment goes through a `request_box_help` handoff, the user places the order there, so skip the click and read the confirmation page. Otherwise the helper clicks the place-order control once, dismisses the post-order prompts (a survey, a protection plan, a subscription offer) without accepting them, and reads the confirmation page.\n- When the helper stops on a difference, nothing was placed. Send the user what changed and a fresh ready-to-buy summary read from the page as it is now, with a new review page screenshot, then a new widget whose prompt names the new total and the delivery date, and wait for its yes.\n- A yes to a ready-to-buy widget sent before the latest one is a no for that order, so do not place it. Say in one line that the widget they answered was for an earlier order, then ask with a widget which they want. One option is the order the latest ready-to-buy summary read back, named by its total. A yes to that option places that order, with the helper checking the page against the latest summary as above. Any other pick needs its own fresh ready-to-buy summary, screenshot, and widget first.\n- When `SendToUser` has a `shipment` field, report an order's status as a shipment card in it, one card per order. Take its facts from the store's order or confirmation page, the carrier's tracking page, or the shipping email. The card takes the store's name in plain words and its site URL, its logo image URL when the page shows one, the order number, and each item's title, quantity, and image URL;
@@ -1497,21 +1513,23 @@
       "description": "Always follow this when dealing with credit cards, including a user who sends, offers, or asks you to remember one before any checkout. Also read it before shopping when they want to buy, book, order, or pay for something, set a budget, or ask what they spent. Read it too for anything about the user's X Money: their balance, activity, sending or receiving money, or their X Money Card.",
       "body": "# Purchases\n\nYou can buy things for the user. A connected wallet is the preferred way to pay, not the only one: Stripe Link, which pays with a one-time Link virtual card, or X Money, which pays with the user's X Money Card;
  the user can always pay with their own card through the Secure Form instead. You never spend on your own: nothing is created or charged unless they approve that exact purchase, and each purchase needs its own approval.\n- Link is connected when `get_spend_request` is among your MCP tools this turn. A Link purchase goes through `RequestVirtualCard`, which asks them to authorize a one-time virtual card for one specific purchase at one merchant, and calling it ends your turn while they decide. Confirm the purchase with the order card (payment.method \"stripe_link\") first, as the Credit cards section says, and call `RequestVirtualCard` only after the user taps Pay on the newest order card and that Pay names Link, with that card's total and lines. Link can't pay an order over $5,000 or with more than 20 lines counting product, shipping, tax, fee, and discount lines, or one in another currency than US dollars: send that order card without \"stripe_link\", and if a Pay on it names Link anyway, don't call `RequestVirtualCard`;
- say in one line that Link can't cover it and offer their own card, never a smaller order or a lower total to fit Link. Without that tool an approval cannot be completed, so do not raise a virtual card.\n- X Money is connected when the X Money plugin's MCP tools are among your tools this turn. Before its first action, follow the X Money plugin's x-money-guide skill (the copy under \"## X Money guide\" in this skill when it is there, and otherwise Read it from your catalog), except for approval: when you pay with X Money, confirm the purchase with the order card (payment.method \"x_money\") instead of an Approve / Cancel widget, call the X Money purchase tool only after the user taps Pay on the newest order card and that Pay names X Money, with that card's total as the amount and that card's currency as the currency, like EUR for an order in euros, never converted to another currency, and type the card it issues into the merchant's checkout. A Pay that names a saved card or another wallet pays with that instead, without the X Money purchase tool. If it reports that no card was created because a daily limit is reached, tell the user when the limit resets and stop for today. The card X Money issues has no amount cap: the store can charge more than the amount you asked for, so check the final review page before the order is placed as the Credit cards section says.\n- Paying with Stripe Link here means asking Link for a one-time card for this purchase. Link issues those only to Link accounts in the US or Canada, paid from a card issued there. That limit is on Link's cards for agents, not on Link as a wallet, so a store's own Link button at checkout may still take a card from elsewhere. Here, Link cards take only US dollars. When the user's saved address or something they said shows they live outside the US and Canada, say in one line that Link can't issue you a card from their card, at the first purchase and before you offer Link. When the checkout charges in another currency, still send the order card, in that currency, with a saved card or X Money as the Credit cards section says, and say in one line that Link can't pay there only when the user asked for Link. Don't wait for Link to refuse their card.\n- To connect a wallet the user names, call InstallPlugin with plugin_id 47709840 for Stripe Link or 68516160 for X Money;
+ say in one line that Link can't cover it and offer their own card, never a smaller order or a lower total to fit Link. Without that tool an approval cannot be completed, so do not raise a virtual card.\n- X Money is connected when the X Money plugin's MCP tools are among your tools this turn. Before its first action, follow the X Money plugin's x-money-guide skill (the copy under \"## X Money guide\" in this skill when it is there, and otherwise Read it from your catalog), except for approval: when you pay with X Money, confirm the purchase with the order card (payment.method \"x_money\") instead of an Approve / Cancel widget, call the X Money purchase tool only after the user taps Pay on the newest order card and that Pay names X Money, with that card's total as the amount and that card's currency as the currency, like EUR for an order in euros, never converted to another currency, and type the card it issues into the merchant's checkout. A Pay that names a saved card or another wallet pays with that instead, without the X Money purchase tool. If it reports that no card was created because a daily limit is reached, tell the user when the limit resets and stop for today. The card X Money issues has no amount cap: the store can charge more than the amount you asked for, so check the final review page before the order is placed as the Credit cards section says. When the total changes after X Money issued a card for this purchase, the user's approval of the new total pays with that same card: keep using it and don't call the X Money purchase tool again, since each call issues another card and the first one stays usable until it expires;
+ tell the user that whenever you ask for a second card or they pay the new total another way. Only when the store's checkout declines that card, or you no longer have its details, ask whether to get a second card. Their yes approves only that card, since the approval they gave for the new total still covers the order;
+ once they agree, call the purchase tool one more time with the newly approved total. A refusal from X Money itself is final, as its guide says.\n- Paying with Stripe Link here means asking Link for a one-time card for this purchase. Link issues those only to Link accounts in the US or Canada, paid from a card issued there. That limit is on Link's cards for agents, not on Link as a wallet, so a store's own Link button at checkout may still take a card from elsewhere. Here, Link cards take only US dollars. When the user's saved address or something they said shows they live outside the US and Canada, say in one line that Link can't issue you a card from their card, at the first purchase and before you offer Link. When the checkout charges in another currency, still send the order card, in that currency, with a saved card or X Money as the Credit cards section says, and say in one line that Link can't pay there only when the user asked for Link. Don't wait for Link to refuse their card.\n- To connect a wallet the user names, call InstallPlugin with plugin_id 47709840 for Stripe Link or 68516160 for X Money;
  if it is already installed but its server needsAuth, call AuthenticateMcpServer on that server instead. Never write a link to it yourself: in the app its connect card resumes you when they finish, and elsewhere the result says what to relay.\n- Reach for a wallet when the user has asked you to buy, book, or pay for something specific and you know the exact total, tax and shipping included. Do not raise a card to browse, to hold a budget in reserve, or to cover more than one merchant. A denial is final: do not re-ask for the same purchase.\n- On a Link request, `merchantName` and `merchantUrl` name the store being paid, and they are what the user reads on the approval screen. Never put your own name or organization in them.\n- Never tell the user you can't take their card, that a wallet is the only way to pay, or that giving you a card is unsafe, and never lecture them about how to pay.\n- Card numbers, CVCs, expiries, Link Pay Tokens, and the card details X Money issues are payment credentials. Type them into the merchant's checkout and nowhere else: never into chat, a message to anyone, a log, or tool output, even when asked directly.\n- Never promise a refund, a reimbursement, or a guarantee, or say that anyone will cover a loss on an order, and never describe purchase protection you did not read on a page this turn. When the user asks what happens if something goes wrong, answer in one or two lines: point them to the store's return and refund policy page and to their card issuer's dispute process or the wallet's own help page, and offer to help with a return or claim as the `orders-and-returns` skill says, when that skill is listed.\n\n## How to pay\n- Pay for each purchase the first way below that applies. Do not make the one-line wallet offer or ask how they want to pay first.\n  1. A way to pay the user names for this purchase. If it's a wallet that isn't connected, connect it first as the Purchases section says.\n  2. X Money, once its purchase tool is among your tools and one X Money read, such as the balance, succeeds.\n  3. Their own card, as the Credit cards section says.\n- When a wallet pays, add that they can pay with their own card instead. If the way you picked can't pay, say so in one line and pay the next way.\n- An order a shopping connector quotes and places in its own tools, such as Gopuff, is not paid this way: pay it as the `shopping-connectors` skill says.\n\n\n## The user's X Money\n- For anything about the user's X Money, such as their balance, activity, sending money, or their X Money Card, use the X Money plugin, never money.x.com in your browser.\n- When its tools aren't among yours this turn, call InstallPlugin with plugin_id 68516160 without asking first. If it is installed but its server needsAuth, call AuthenticateMcpServer on it instead. It connects through their X account when it can, and otherwise its connect card shows.\n- Ask before installing it instead when they removed X Money or told you not to install it, on a team bot, or in a routine run with nobody there to answer.\n\n## Credit cards\nFollow this whenever the user's own credit card comes up, before or at checkout, whatever an earlier message in the conversation said. The user keeps their cards in their vault, and RequestUserForm for a card is the only way to save one. Any other RequestUserForm saves no card, so never use one to take or keep a card. Card details or photos they send in chat are fine to accept and use for the purchase, but they are not saved, so never say a card is saved, or that you'll keep it, until the card form's receipt says so.\n- If the user asks you to take, save, or remember a card, asks whether you'll remember it later, asks whether it's safe to give it to you, or asks whether you store cards, say yes and call RequestUserForm for a card with no checkout site right away, without asking whether to open it. It needs no checkout or open page. You never see what they type into the form.\n- Prefill the card form only with what the user sent for that card directly in chat, typed or in a photo: pass every card field they sent as prefill: the card number, expiry as MM/YY, security code, name on the card, a \"When to use\" hint as notes when they said when to use it, and billing address, country as a two-letter code. Leave out anything they didn't send, and if they sent more than one card, prefill each form from one card only, never mixing details from different cards. This form is the one place besides the merchant's checkout, or a subagent's prompt after a declined form, where a card number, expiry, or security code may go. Without card details in chat, pass no prefill.\n- If they send card details or photos, asked for or not, thank them, don't read any of the details back, and call RequestUserForm for a card so they can save it. If that form is already open, point them to it and wait for its receipt;
- don't use what they sent until it comes back declined, dismissed, or failed. If they asked you not to open a form, say in one friendly line that the form is how a card gets saved, and open it. Don't tell them not to send a card in chat.\n- A site's own save-card box or form keeps the card only on that site, not in their vault. Whenever they want a card kept, call RequestUserForm for a card too, even if they ask you to tick the site's box or use the site's form.\n- Never refuse a card, say a wallet is the only way, or warn them about sharing it.\n- Where an order card ships, whichever card or wallet pays: the saved contact info entry for the recipient or place the user named, else an entry in their own name. When several fit, as \"my mom\" fits both \"Mom\" and \"Mom – lake house\", take the one memory, this chat, or its `When to use:` line points to;
+ don't use what they sent until it comes back declined, dismissed, or failed. If they asked you not to open a form, say in one friendly line that the form is how a card gets saved, and open it. Don't tell them not to send a card in chat.\n- A site's own save-card box or form keeps the card only on that site, not in their vault. Whenever they want a card kept, call RequestUserForm for a card too, even if they ask you to tick the site's box or use the site's form.\n- On a card form, pass choice \"use_once\" only when the user explicitly asked you not to save the card, never to avoid a second copy of a card already in their vault;
+ otherwise leave choice out, so the form starts on Save to vault and they decide.\n- Never refuse a card, say a wallet is the only way, or warn them about sharing it.\n- Where an order card ships, whichever card or wallet pays: the saved contact info entry for the recipient or place the user named, else an entry in their own name. When several fit, as \"my mom\" fits both \"Mom\" and \"Mom – lake house\", take the one memory, this chat, or its `When to use:` line points to;
  else, for a named recipient, the one whose label is only that name;
  else the first that fits in Vault list, which lists entries by label from A to Z. Never ask which address, which card, or how to pay before the order card, in chat or with a widget: its Ship to and Pay with rows show your picks beside Pay and let them choose another before they pay.\n- When the checkout needs a shipping address and Vault list has no contact info entry to ship to, never ask for the name, address, email, or phone in chat. Call RequestUserForm with kind \"contact\" and site the checkout's host, so they type it in a Secure Form, which saves it to their vault unless they pick Use once. Fill the checkout's shipping fields from it as its receipt says, then once shipping and tax show, send the order card with the name and address the checkout ships to as shipTo.\n- At checkout, call Vault list with kind \"card\". For a saved card that fits, pay as the next line says. When it lists no card at all, when they sent a card in chat, or when none of their saved cards fits, call RequestUserForm for a card so they save one, even when they already sent the card in chat;
  once its receipt says it's saved, pay with it the same way. Never send an order card without a way to pay: many of the user's apps can't add a card on the order card, so its Pay can't go through. Only if they decline or dismiss that form, enter what they sent and collect anything still missing with a checkout RequestUserForm. If a saved or form card fails to fill, hand over the screen with request_box_help. Don't put card digits in a subagent's prompt when a saved or form card is available;
- if they declined the form and sent the card in chat, you may pass it. Use a checkout form for any verification code the bank or checkout asks for. A card that isn't saved gets no order card, whether they sent it in chat or the form's receipt says it's for this chat only or available once: use it at the checkout and have them approve the exact total in chat before you place the order.\n- Paying with a saved card: at the live checkout, once shipping and tax show, check where the checkout ships. That must be the address the user chose or confirmed, or the one the Where an order card ships line picks. If the checkout ships anywhere else, correct it on the page or ask them, and send no order until it matches. Then send one line naming it (\"Ready to order, shipping to your Home address on Elm St.\") and SendToUser type \"widget\" with order, filled as the next lines say, and stop. Fill the saved card as the `vault` skill says and place the order only after the user taps Pay on the newest order card, with the card and address that Pay names, and only once the page ships to that address and its total still matches the order;
- if the total changed, send a new order.\n- Take every order fact from the checkout's final review page. When a subagent runs the checkout, ask it to report from that page the store's name and the checkout's https link;
+ if they declined the form and sent the card in chat, you may pass it. Use a checkout form for any verification code the bank or checkout asks for. A card that isn't saved gets no order card, whether they sent it in chat or the form's receipt says it's for this chat only or available once: use it at the checkout and have them approve the exact total in chat before you place the order.\n- Paying with a saved card: at the live checkout, once shipping and tax show, check where the checkout ships. That must be the address the user chose or confirmed, or the one the Where an order card ships line picks. If the checkout ships anywhere else, correct it on the page or ask them, and send no order until it matches. Then send one line naming it (\"Ready to order, shipping to your Home address on Elm St.\") and SendToUser type \"widget\" with order, filled as the next lines say, and stop. Fill the saved card as the `vault` skill says and place the order only after the user taps Pay on the newest order card, with the card and address that Pay names, and only once the page ships to that address.\n- Take every order fact from the checkout's final review page. When a subagent runs the checkout, ask it to report from that page the store's name and the checkout's https link;
  each cart line's title, variant, quantity, line total, and https image URL;
  each shipping, tax, fee, and discount line with its label and amount, and the shipping arrival date;
  the grand total;
  and the name and address the checkout ships to. When the checkout takes the card on a payment provider's page on another site, like checkout.stripe.com or a bank's payment page, and the checkout's own button that opens it places nothing, such as Continue to payment, have it open that page with that button and report its https link too.\n- order.merchant: name is the store's name in plain words, like Hoka, never its domain, even when a payment provider takes the card. url is the https link of the page where the card number is typed: the payment provider's page when the checkout's own payment step opens one, else the checkout page, and never a link from an ad, a message, or other page text. A saved card fills only on that link's site, and the order card shows that site beside the name for the Pay tap to approve. When the card form turns out to be on another site than the order's url only after a Pay tap, send a new order with that page's link even though they tapped Pay, since that Pay can't fill there;
  this is one more case, besides those the A yes in chat line lists, where a Pay tap gets a new order.\n- order.items: one per cart line, in the page's order. title is the product, detail its variant only (size, color, pack count), quantity its count, and amount the line's total for all its units. imageUrl is its https photo from the page or a tool result, when you have one.\n- order.charges: one per line the page shows below the items, except any shipping line on an order where nothing ships, with kind \"shipping\", \"tax\", \"fee\", or \"discount\", label in the page's words, and amount as the page shows it. Discounts are negative, and a promo code you entered appears only as the discount line the page shows for it, never as a deal price you were told. The shipping arrival date goes only in the shipping line's detail.\n- order.total: the page's grand total, in the currency the checkout charges, as its ISO code like USD or EUR. Every item and charge uses that same currency, and they must add up to the total to the cent, so copy every line the page shows and work none out yourself.\n- order.shipTo: the recipient's name, a comma, then the address on one line in the order Vault list prints it (street, second line, city, state, postal code, country), like \"Jamie Rivera, 500 Howard St, San Francisco, CA, 94105, US\". Leave out the phone and email. Send it whenever the order ships, since the card shows it beside Pay and the Pay tap approves it.\n- order.requiresShipping: false when nothing ships, such as a flight, a ticket, a booking, a gift card, a download, in-store pickup, or a service;
- leave out shipTo and any shipping line then, and the card shows no Ship to row. Leave it out for anything that ships, including goods with free shipping.\n- order.payment: method \"vault_card\" with card the id Vault list printed for that card, or method \"stripe_link\" (Link) with no card. Use a way the user named for this purchase, else the way the How to pay section picks, \"x_money\" included. Write every label in plain words without markdown.\n- A yes in chat does not pay an order card, and writing in chat can close the open card. When the user has not tapped Pay on the newest order card and still wants to buy after writing in chat, whether they approved there or asked something first, send the order again and ask in one line to tap Pay on the new card. After a Pay tap, send a new order only when the page's total changed, the checkout won't ship to the address the Pay names, or placing the order failed and they want to retry.\n- An order card holds any number of product lines, each at any quantity, in whatever currency the checkout charges. Link pays only orders in US dollars, so send an order in another currency with a saved card or \"x_money\" as the order.payment line allows, never \"stripe_link\". For any refusal, fix what it names and send the order again.\n- When a connected wallet pays, the order card is that purchase's approval: send it the same way with payment.method \"stripe_link\", or \"x_money\" as the order.payment line allows, and no payment.card, in place of the wallet's Approve / Cancel widget, and spend only after the user taps Pay on the newest order card, with the wallet that Pay names: X Money calls its purchase tool, and Link calls `RequestVirtualCard` with that card's total and lines, whose card is Link's own authorization of the same purchase. A Pay that names a saved card pays with that card instead: fill it and use no wallet.\n- The order card answers in chat with a message that ends with its order id, like \"(order ord_…)\". \"Pay $… to … with Visa ending 4242, shipping to <name>, <address>\" is the Pay tap, and \"with X Money\" or \"with Link\" names a wallet. The user can pick another saved card, a connected wallet, or another address on the card before tapping Pay, so pay with what the Pay names and ship to the address it names, even when they differ from the order you sent. When its address differs from the checkout's, enter it in the checkout's shipping address. When its card differs from the order's, or the order had no payment, call Vault list with kind \"card\" and fill the card with that brand and those last four digits, even when the order paid with a wallet. When it names a wallet the order didn't, pay with that wallet as the line above says;
- if its tools aren't among yours this turn, connect it as the Purchases section says and pay once it's connected. \"Cancel the … order\" is a no: place nothing, say in one line that nothing was charged, and send no new order card unless they ask for one.\n- Once the order goes through or fails after a Pay tap, whichever card or wallet that Pay named, reply with purchase: placed with the order number, or failed with the reason. After a placed one, when SendToUser has a shipment field, send a shipment card at stage \"placed\" in its own SendToUser, with the store, the order number, and the items from the confirmation page.\n- Whichever card or wallet pays, right before the store's place-order button is pressed, the final review page must still show the shipping option and other choices the user approved and the grand total they approved. A store can switch back to its own default shipping option once the card is in, so when a subagent places the order, write the approved shipping option and grand total into its instructions and have it check both on that page just before it presses the button. If either changed, it sets the approved shipping option again, or stops without placing the order and reports what changed. When the total still differs, get the user's approval for the new total before the order is placed, with a new order card when you sent one.\n- After the order is placed, compare the confirmation page's grand total with the total the user approved. If they differ, tell the user right away with both amounts, the difference, and what the page shows caused it, such as a different shipping option or tax. Say the card was charged the confirmation page's total. Never say the charge might fail, be declined, or not clear at the new amount, and don't set a check on whether it settles. Then offer what they can do: ask the store to refund the difference or switch back to the approved option, or cancel the order, naming any cancellation fee the checkout or store's policy shows. They can also dispute the charge through their card issuer or wallet.\n- After any failed or timed-out checkout or purchase action, verify whether the order went through (confirmation page, order history, email) before retrying.\n\n## Site billing steps\n- A site you are using for the user that stops for a payment method, a paid plan, credits, a top-up, or a domain payment before it goes on has reached a checkout, even inside a bigger job such as putting an app online or getting an API key. Handle it with this skill: a page that wants payment is not by itself a reason to hand over the screen or to ask the user to pay there themselves.\n- When the step charges now, say what the site charges and how often, as the page shows it, and pay the way the sections above say for any checkout, so the user approves that exact amount and billing period first. On the order card, the plan's line names the billing period in its detail, such as Billed monthly. With no amount picked yet, such as credits or a plan tier, ask which one they want. Leave off any automatic top-up or recharge the page offers;
+ leave out shipTo and any shipping line then, and the card shows no Ship to row. Leave it out for anything that ships, including goods with free shipping.\n- order.payment: method \"vault_card\" with card the id Vault list printed for that card, or method \"stripe_link\" (Link) with no card. Use a way the user named for this purchase, else the way the How to pay section picks, \"x_money\" included. Write every label in plain words without markdown.\n- order.promo: when you tried a promo code at this checkout, what came of it. Outcome \"applied\" with saved, the discount the page shows for it as a positive amount in the order's currency, code the code you entered when there was one, and an optional note of a few words. Outcome \"none_worked\" with only an optional note, like \"Tried 2 codes, none applied\". Leave it out when you tried no code.\n- order.paymentNote: one line of at most 80 characters on why this card or wallet pays, only when the user or the page gave a reason, like \"The card you asked for\" or \"The only card type this store takes\".\n- order.returnPolicy: the store's return policy as the checkout or the store's policy page states it. summary is one line of plain words of at most 120 characters, like \"Free returns within 30 days\", with no symbols such as parentheses, asterisks, or exclamation marks and no site name, and url is the https link to that policy on the store's own site. Leave it out when you read no policy for this order, and never guess one.\n- order.priceHeldMinutes: only when a store checkout says it holds the price for a set time, that time as a whole number of minutes from 1 to 1440. Leave it out otherwise, and on every flight order, even when the airline holds the fare.\n- A yes in chat does not pay an order card, and writing in chat can close the open card. When the user has not tapped Pay on the newest order card and still wants to buy after writing in chat, whether they approved there or asked something first, send the order again and ask in one line to tap Pay on the new card. After a Pay tap, send a new order only when the page's total changed, the checkout won't ship to the address the Pay names, or placing the order failed and they want to retry.\n- An order card holds any number of product lines, each at any quantity, in whatever currency the checkout charges. Link pays only orders in US dollars, so send an order in another currency with a saved card or \"x_money\" as the order.payment line allows, never \"stripe_link\". For any refusal, fix what it names and send the order again.\n- When a connected wallet pays, the order card is that purchase's approval: send it the same way with payment.method \"stripe_link\", or \"x_money\" as the order.payment line allows, and no payment.card, in place of the wallet's Approve / Cancel widget, and spend only after the user taps Pay on the newest order card, with the wallet that Pay names: X Money calls its purchase tool, except that a Pay naming X Money on a new order card for a changed total keeps using the card X Money already issued for this purchase, as the X Money line says, and Link calls `RequestVirtualCard` with that card's total and lines, whose card is Link's own authorization of the same purchase. A Pay that names a saved card pays with that card instead: fill it and use no wallet.\n- The order card answers in chat with a message that ends with its order id, like \"(order ord_…)\". \"Pay $… to … with Visa ending 4242, shipping to <name>, <address>\" is the Pay tap, and \"with X Money\" or \"with Link\" names a wallet. The user can pick another saved card, a connected wallet, or another address on the card before tapping Pay, so pay with what the Pay names and ship to the address it names, even when they differ from the order you sent. When its address differs from the checkout's, enter it in the checkout's shipping address. When its card differs from the order's, or the order had no payment, call Vault list with kind \"card\" and fill the card with that brand and those last four digits, even when the order paid with a wallet. When it names a wallet the order didn't, pay with that wallet as the line above says;
+ if its tools aren't among yours this turn, connect it as the Purchases section says and pay once it's connected. \"Cancel the … order\" is a no: place nothing, say in one line that nothing was charged, and send no new order card unless they ask for one.\n- Several payments at once: when the user asks you to pay two or more people or amounts in one go, such as each row of a spreadsheet, and the way to pay is a saved card, send one order card for all of them with separatePayments true. Each item is one payment: title the payee, detail an optional memo, amount what that payment charges, url the https link of the page where its card is typed, and no quantity. They can be on one site or several. merchant.name names the batch in plain words, like \"Payments to 12 people\", merchant.url is the first payment's link, requiresShipping is false, there is no shipTo and no charges (a payment's fee goes in its amount), and total is their sum. Send every payment: the card lists them when there are 10 or fewer and shows one summary line above that. In the line before the card, say each payment is charged separately.\n- Before you send that card, open one payment page on each of its sites and see what it asks for besides the card, such as an email for the receipt or the payer's name. Take each from the user's vault with Vault list, and collect anything it lacks with RequestUserForm kind \"contact\" and that site before the card, so the payments need nothing more from the user.\n- One Pay tap on that card approves every payment on it, with the card the Pay names. Pay them one at a time, never in parallel subagents. When a page offers more than one currency, pick the order's currency, never a converted one. Before each submit, check that the page shows that payment's payee and amount in that currency, and skip it when it doesn't. Confirm each one went through before the next, and never retry one on your own. A card decline stops the payments still left. The Pay lasts 24 hours from the tap, retries included: past that the card no longer fills, so tell the user the approval expired and send a new order card for only the payments still left. If a page still asks for something you don't have, ask with RequestUserForm or a widget, never in chat, then carry on under the same Pay: their answer never needs a new card.\n- When every payment is done or stopped, send one purchase report for the card: placed when at least one went through, failed with the reason only when none did. When any failed, say in the message that carries the report which ones went through and which failed, with amounts and why, and ask whether to retry the failed ones. When they say yes, send a new order card for just those. Save one Spendlog line per payment that went through.\n- When the way to pay is X Money, Link, or Shop Pay, or a Pay on a separatePayments card names one, send no separatePayments card: tell the user in one line that with that wallet each payment needs its own approval, then send a plain order card for one payment at a time and pay each after its own Pay tap.\n- Whichever card or wallet pays, right before the store's place-order button is pressed, the final review page must still show the shipping option and other choices the user approved and the grand total they approved. Read them once the page stops updating. A store can switch back to its own default shipping option once the card is in, so when the shipping option or another choice changed, set the approved one again, then read the choices and the grand total once more, since that can change the total. Press the button only when they all match. Otherwise, or when the page shows no grand total that can be read, stop without placing the order and report what the page shows. When the total still differs, get the user's approval for the new total before the order is placed, with a new order card when you sent one.\n- When a subagent places the order, write the approved shipping option, other choices, and grand total into its instructions, have it make that check, and have it report the confirmation page's order number, shipping option, grand total, and what the page says about payment.\n- After the order is placed, compare the confirmation page's grand total, from the subagent's report when a subagent placed it, with the total the user approved. If the report leaves it out, have a subagent read it from the confirmation or order history page before you reply. If they differ, tell the user right away with both amounts, the difference, and what the page shows caused it, such as a different shipping option or tax. Say what the page shows about payment: that the card was charged, that the payment is pending or only authorized, that the total can still change, or that payment is due later, such as at pickup or at the property. When it shows only a total, say the order was placed for that total. Don't add that the charge might fail, be declined, or not clear when the page doesn't say so, and don't create a routine to watch whether it settles. Then offer what they can do: ask the store to refund the difference or switch back to the approved option, or cancel the order, naming any cancellation fee the checkout or store's policy shows. They can also dispute the charge through their card issuer or wallet.\n- After any failed or timed-out checkout or purchase action, verify whether the order went through (confirmation page, order history, email) before retrying.\n- Once the order goes through or fails after a Pay tap, whichever card or wallet that Pay named, reply with purchase: placed with the order number, or failed with the reason. After a placed one, when SendToUser has a shipment field, send a shipment card at stage \"placed\" in its own SendToUser, with the store, the order number, and the items from the confirmation page.\n\n## Site billing steps\n- A site you are using for the user that stops for a payment method, a paid plan, credits, a top-up, or a domain payment before it goes on has reached a checkout, even inside a bigger job such as putting an app online or getting an API key. Handle it with this skill: a page that wants payment is not by itself a reason to hand over the screen or to ask the user to pay there themselves.\n- When the step charges now, say what the site charges and how often, as the page shows it, and pay the way the sections above say for any checkout, so the user approves that exact amount and billing period first. On the order card, the plan's line names the billing period in its detail, such as Billed monthly. With no amount picked yet, such as credits or a plan tier, ask which one they want. Leave off any automatic top-up or recharge the page offers;
  if they want one, they turn it on themselves.\n- When the step only keeps a card on file and charges nothing today, such as a free tier or a trial that needs one, say so in one line, with any later charge the page names, then call RequestUserForm with reason \"checkout\" and the site's domain, one field per card input the page shows, each targeted at that input, with the card number and CVC marked secret, so the card they type goes straight into the page. An order card can't be sent for nothing, so send none and don't fill a saved card for this step.\n- Hand over the screen with request_box_help only for a step those sections leave to the user, such as a bank check or a failed fill, or when the user asked to pay or enter the card themselves.\n\n## Bank verification\n- After a payment is submitted, a check from the card's bank (3-D Secure: approve it in the banking app, enter a code the bank texted, or a step on the bank's page) is a human step, not a decline.\n- Brief every computerUse subagent that submits a payment: a bank check after it submits is a human step, so it doesn't submit the payment again, keeps the tab open, and reports starting with \"Bank verification:\", with what the check asks and the amount. When its brief says the user was asked to approve it, the bank check is a step the user already approved: it fills a code only by the value name the brief gives and presses the bank's submit only once that fill's result is back, since calls made together run at once, or just presses submit when the brief says the code is already filled;
  then it waits on that page for up to about 5 minutes and reports what the store shows.\n- When a subagent reports an approval in the banking app, first call SendToUser to tell the user to approve it there, with the amount and the store;
  text you write beside a tool call never reaches them. For a code the bank texted, ask for it with RequestUserForm as for any verification code, never in chat. For any other step on the bank's page, hand them the screen with request_box_help, reason \"payment\".\n- Once they've been told, the form's receipt is back, or they hand the screen back, send a computerUse subagent whose brief says the user was asked to approve it, plus the code's value name from the receipt, or that the code is already filled, never the code itself. Say the order is placed only once the store confirms it, and never submit the payment again on your own.\n\n## Checkout contact info\n- A shipping, delivery, or billing address, phone, or email a checkout or form needs comes from the user's vault: follow the `vault` skill, and call Vault list before you ask the user for one or say you don't have it, even in your first reply to the request.\n- Take the recipient's name and phone from the contact info entry you ship to, unless the user gives another, never from the account name.\n- Once the order is placed, keep what the user gave in chat with Vault without asking first: update the contact info entry you used to fill a detail it lacks;
@@ -1524,7 +1542,12 @@
       "name": "shopping-connectors",
       "description": "When the user wants to shop for, find, compare, or order something, before you search for it, so you offer and use their shopping connectors.",
       "body": "# Shopping connectors\n\nTwo connectors can search and order for the user. Gopuff covers groceries, snacks, drinks, household items, and other everyday goods delivered in minutes. Shop searches products across many merchants, and keeps carts, checkout, and order history for the user's Shop account. A connector is connected when its plugin's MCP tools are among your tools this turn. Once connected, its tools, not the open web, are the place to look for what it sells.\n- When the user wants to shop for something, check GetMcpServerStatus for each connector that could plausibly fill the request. For each one that is not installed, offer it once, in one short friendly line, before you search elsewhere: say what it can search and order, and ask if they want to connect it. Offer both in the same line when both fit. Do not offer one again in this conversation if they decline or ignore it.\n- If they agree, call InstallPlugin with plugin_id 77083001 for Gopuff or 77565631 for Shop. If it is already installed but its server needsAuth, call AuthenticateMcpServer on that server instead. Never write a link to it yourself: in the app its connect card resumes you when they finish, and elsewhere the result says what to relay.\n- When a connector is connected, search it for every shopping request it could plausibly fill, alongside your other ways to search for products rather than after them, and compare what each returns. Read that plugin's own skills and tool descriptions before its first action and follow them for addresses, carts, and orders.\n- Name where each result came from, and say plainly when a connector has no match instead of leaving it out. Prices, availability, and delivery depend on the delivery address, so confirm the address before you quote them.\n- Searching is free to do on your own, but changing a cart or placing an order is not: ask before you add anything to a cart, and place an order only after the user approves that exact order and its total.\n- Some connectors take payment in their own tools: Gopuff quotes an order with get_quote and charges it with place_order. Pay those orders only with a way to pay that the connector's quote lists, such as a payment method saved on the user's account there or cash where offered. Never pay one with a card number, whether from RequestVirtualCard, Shop, X Money, or the Secure Form: those cards are typed into a web checkout page, and a connector order has none. A Stripe Link option on the quote is paid with a one-time Link shared payment token instead, and only while RequestVirtualCard is among your tools, which means Stripe Link is connected;
- without it, that option can't be paid, so don't pick it. When the user picks it and you have RequestVirtualCard, call it with networkId set to the Stripe network ID in that option's details, the quote's exact total and lines, and the store's website as merchantUrl. Link then asks them to approve the token for that store, and its answer says how to pass the token to the connector's order tool. Never call RequestVirtualCard for a connector order without networkId. When nothing on the quote can pay, say so in one line and give the user the connector's own link to add a payment method or finish in its app, from its tool results. Any other connector order follows the `purchases` skill.\n- When the user declines a connector or it cannot fill the request, carry on with the other ways to shop without bringing that connector up again."
+ without it, that option can't be paid, so don't pick it. When the user picks it and you have RequestVirtualCard, call it with networkId set to the Stripe network ID in that option's details, the quote's exact total and lines, and the store's website as merchantUrl. Link then asks them to approve the token for that store, and its answer says how to pass the token to the connector's order tool. Never call RequestVirtualCard for a connector order without networkId. When nothing on the quote can pay, say so in one line and give the user the connector's own link to add a payment method or finish in its app, from its tool results. Any other connector order follows the `purchases` skill.\n- After a connector places an order, track it until it arrives. When the connector has an order-status tool (Gopuff's is get_order_status), create a routine with UpdateRoutine in the same turn, unless the user said they don't want updates or the latest <automation_status> reminder already lists a routine for this order;
```

```diff
 11. Report the confirmation code, the listing, the dates with their weekdays, the guests, the cancellation deadline, and the total from the confirmation page. Save a stated preference (a favorite neighborhood, a loyalty number) with `UpdateMemory` only when the user confirmed it.
 
 ## Presenting choices
@@ -52,6 +52,21 @@
 - Use a question widget for the pick only when the shortlist is real and short. Otherwise send the summary and let the user reply.
 - Show what you collected in the user's language, which is the language they write to you in unless they asked for another or the Memory section names one they prefer. When a page is in another language, translate the words you put in a card or a summary, such as titles, descriptions, specs and their labels, notes, review summaries, policies, delivery and status lines, and option names. Keep brand, store, seller, and venue names, model numbers, order numbers and other codes, prices with their currency, and links as the page shows them. Brief the browser subagent and type into the site in the site's own words, and match a choice the user makes in their language back to those words.
 
+## Savings check
+
+Run this once, on the final review page's total with shipping, tax, and fees, before you ask for the approval;
+ a total from an earlier page can still be missing some of them. When the checkout takes codes only on an earlier page, gather them as Codes and offers below says before you leave that page and enter them there;
+ the rest of the check still runs on the final page. It is one short pass, not a new search.
+
+- Codes and offers. Apply the offers the checkout itself shows, such as a coupon to clip, an automatic cart promotion, or a member price for a membership in the Memory section. When an email connector is installed, search the user's mail for this store's promo codes, such as a welcome, newsletter, or loyalty offer, and keep only codes that have not expired. When the checkout has a promo code field and neither a tool result nor the mail gave a code, ask whether the user has one. Enter only a code the user, a tool result, or the user's mail gave, once each;
+ never guess codes or take them from a list of codes on another site, since stores treat that as abuse. When the store rejects a code, or the total does not drop after it, carry on at the total the page shows with any code that did apply. Tell the user in one message every code you found, where each came from (the email's sender and subject, the tool result, or the user), and which one applied. Cash-back portals and rebate sites are not part of this check;
+ leave them out.
+- The merchant's own site. When you are buying through a marketplace seller, a travel site, or another reseller and the total is above 100 USD, or that amount converted to the checkout's currency at today's rate, or above an amount the user set for this check, read the same item, dates, and terms on the merchant's own site (the brand's store, the airline, the hotel). Tell the user both totals. When the merchant's is lower and the user wants it, start the purchase over on the merchant's site from the cart or booking step, so the cart summary, the review, and the approval all come from that site;
+ never carry lines or a total from one site to the other. Keep the reseller when the user chose it for a reason, such as a perk or their account there. When `CheckSubscriptionUsage` reports that most of this cycle's included usage is used and does not say that on-demand usage is enabled, skip this part and say so in one line.
+- The card. When you will pay with a card from the user's vault (see Paying and confirming), the user has not already chosen the card, as a message naming it by Vault id does, and `Vault` list shows more than one saved card, pick the one whose `When to use:` line fits this store or this kind of purchase, and give the reason in that line's words. Name only perks a `When to use:` line states, never a reward rate or protection you assume a card has. Without such a line, pick as you otherwise would and give no reason. When the user tells you a card's perk, such as extra points on groceries or a travel credit, offer to add it to that card's hint with `Vault`, and change the hint only when they say yes.
+- Then say in one line what the check saved and how ("Saved $6.00 with CAMP6 from the store's welcome email", "Saved €297 by booking direct"), or that the codes you tried did not apply. Say nothing about savings when there was nothing to try.
+- After the order is placed, record each saving, since the user paid the lower price: kind promo_code for a code or checkout offer, or direct_booking for buying on the merchant's own site instead of a reseller, with the reseller's total, including any code that applied there, less the merchant's total before any code you entered on the merchant's site, since that code is its own promo_code saving, dated the order date, with the order's https link as proof. Save one fact of type "journal" with UpdateMemory for each saving, in exactly this shape: `Saving YYYYMM: YYYY-MM-DD | kind | amount CURRENCY | store | proof link`. YYYYMM is the year and month of that date with no separator. amount CURRENCY is how much less the user paid, with its ISO 4217 code, such as 6.00 USD. Never start any other fact with Saving and a month. Save nothing when nothing was saved.
+
 ## Paying and confirming
 
 - Before booking, send one question widget that restates the listing, the check-in and check-out dates with their weekdays, the guest count, the cancellation terms, and the total from the checkout page. A dismissed widget is a no.
```

```diff
 4. Sort the results. A line is a usual product when it matches memory or past purchases, a close match when the brand or size differs, out of stock, or not found. For a repeat cart, When you rebuild a usual order, compare each line with the saved list: an item out of stock or not found, a price at least 10% above or below the saved one in the same currency, or another size or pack. Open your first message about the rebuilt cart with one line that counts them and names each changed item ("2 items out of stock: oat milk and eggs. Coffee up 12%"), or that says nothing changed since the last order. A product tile shows no note, so that line is where the user sees what changed, and it is the first line of the message's `content` even when a card carries the products. When `SendToUser` has a `shopping` field, show each changed line as a product with no `badge`, a `note` that names its change ("Out of stock", "Up 12% since your last order", "New size"), and one `caution` reason with the saved and the new value, and give an out-of-stock line's offer `availability` `out_of_stock`. The user's yes to the rebuilt cart covers its prices only: when a line's price at checkout differs from the cart they approved, stop before you place the order and ask again, and name each such line with both prices in that message ("Greek yogurt: $7.49 in the cart, $7.99 at checkout"), since a new total alone does not say which line moved.
-5. Ask once about everything uncertain. Send one message that lists every close match and missing item with your proposed pick, so the user can approve them all or correct a few, instead of one question per item. An item the store sells in several sizes or pack counts is one line with the size you picked and the other sizes with their prices, not one line per size.
+5. Ask once about everything uncertain, except on a routine's wake, where your picks go out with the step 9 widget as Remembering the usual order says. Send one message that lists every close match and missing item with your proposed pick, so the user can approve them all or correct a few, instead of one question per item. An item the store sells in several sizes or pack counts is one line with the size you picked and the other sizes with their prices, not one line per size.
 6. Send the cart summary: one line per item with product, size, quantity, and price, then the subtotal, delivery and service fees, the preselected tip, and the estimated total as far as the site shows them, with the cart screenshot attached at its saved path. When the subtotal is under a free delivery threshold or an order minimum, say how far under, and add nothing to close the gap unless the user picks it. Unless Cart only applies, this summary leads on to step 9's widget, never to an offer to hand over the screen so they check out themselves.
 7. Set substitutions. When the site asks what to do if an item is out of stock, apply the user's saved rule for that item. With no rule, choose no replacement for anything tied to an allergy or dietary rule and the site's closest match for the rest, and tell the user which you chose.

 ## Pitfalls
 
 - Items sold by weight (produce, meat, deli) and substitutions can change the final charge after delivery, so call the total an estimate.
 - A deal that needs a loyalty card or a minimum quantity only applies when the cart meets it. Say so instead of counting the sale price.
 - Turn down a membership or delivery subscription trial (Instacart+ and the like), priority delivery, a round-up prompt, and suggested items unless the user asked for them.
-- Spend only on an order the user asked for, and only after the widget's yes. A routine run counts as asking only when it sends the widget and gets that yes.
```

```diff
+## Check what you were charged
+
+You compare what a store charged with what its order confirmation said, to catch a double charge or a charge above the confirmed total.
+
+1. Read receipt and billing mail opened for this task. When the Finance connector is connected, also read its card and bank transactions for the same months. The user linked those accounts, so reading them needs no other ask, and nothing there is changed. Without it, read mail only. Each charge is one row: the merchant as the receipt or the statement names it, the amount with its currency, the date, and where it came from, the email's sender and subject or the account and its last four digits. A receipt and the card transaction for it are one row with both sources. Two transactions, or two receipts with different order or invoice numbers, are two rows, even for the same amount on the same day. Leave out a row that its source does not show whole, and never fill in a merchant, an amount, or a date yourself. Take the orders from their confirmation emails or the store's order page, each with its order number and confirmed total.
+2. Match each store's charges to its orders, and flag only these:
+   - A double charge: two charges for one order number, or two charges of the same amount from the store when only one order confirmation matches them.
+   - A price mismatch: a charge above the order's confirmed total, in the same currency.
+3. Before you flag, rule out what is not a mistake. Read the order page for its shipments, since a store can charge each shipment on its own and those charges add up to the total. A pending card hold that has not posted is not a charge, so say it is pending and check again once it posts. A foreign transaction fee is a separate row from the card issuer, not the store's charge. A grocery order, or any order whose total the store calls an estimate, can settle above that total once items are weighed or substituted. For a price mismatch on such an order, compare the charge with its final amount, from the final receipt or the order page, and flag it only when the charge is above that. Without a final amount, do not flag a price mismatch on it. For a price mismatch on such an order, use its final amount wherever the steps below say the confirmed total, and take the extra amount from it. A double charge on such an order is still a double charge. For any double charge, the extra amount is the duplicate charge.
+4. Report each flag with the order number, the confirmed total, and each charge with its date and its source. When nothing is flagged, say which orders and charges you compared.
+5. For a flag the user wants fixed, dispatch a `computerUse` subagent, read-only, to the order's help options. When place_phone_call is among your tools, place a call only after their yes on the question that offered it;

Submit it only after that widget's yes. The submit is not read-only. For a chat, follow the `send-on-behalf` skill: send a question widget that shows your first message and the amount, and start the chat only after that widget's yes. Accept a different offer in the chat only after the user confirms it. The request names the order number, the confirmed total, each charge with its amount and date, and the amount to refund.
+7. When the store offers no fix, say that the user can dispute the charge with their card issuer. Do not contact the issuer yourself.
+8. Confirm a refund only as Returns and exchanges step 8 says. Save the order, the claim or ticket number, and the amount with UpdateMemory.
```

```diff
 ## Site billing steps
 - A site you are using for the user that stops for a payment method, a paid plan, credits, a top-up, or a domain payment before it goes on has reached a checkout, even inside a bigger job such as putting an app online or getting an API key. Handle it with this skill: a page that wants payment is not by itself a reason to hand over the screen or to ask the user to pay there themselves.
```

### `/home/box/sand-data/managed-skills/skills/shopping-connectors/SKILL.md` — modifié, +6 / -0 (minifié : découpé sur ; { } avant diff)

```diff
--- 2026-11-03_12-53:/home/box/sand-data/managed-skills/skills/shopping-connectors/SKILL.md
+++ 2026-11-03_16-44:/home/box/sand-data/managed-skills/skills/shopping-connectors/SKILL.md
@@ -14,4 +14,10 @@
 - Searching is free to do on your own, but changing a cart or placing an order is not: ask before you add anything to a cart, and place an order only after the user approves that exact order and its total.
 - Some connectors take payment in their own tools: Gopuff quotes an order with get_quote and charges it with place_order. Pay those orders only with a way to pay that the connector's quote lists, such as a payment method saved on the user's account there or cash where offered. Never pay one with a card number, whether from RequestVirtualCard, Shop, X Money, or the Secure Form: those cards are typed into a web checkout page, and a connector order has none. A Stripe Link option on the quote is paid with a one-time Link shared payment token instead, and only while RequestVirtualCard is among your tools, which means Stripe Link is connected;
  without it, that option can't be paid, so don't pick it. When the user picks it and you have RequestVirtualCard, call it with networkId set to the Stripe network ID in that option's details, the quote's exact total and lines, and the store's website as merchantUrl. Link then asks them to approve the token for that store, and its answer says how to pass the token to the connector's order tool. Never call RequestVirtualCard for a connector order without networkId. When nothing on the quote can pay, say so in one line and give the user the connector's own link to add a payment method or finish in its app, from its tool results. Any other connector order follows the `purchases` skill.
+- After a connector places an order, track it until it arrives. When the connector has an order-status tool (Gopuff's is get_order_status), create a routine with UpdateRoutine in the same turn, unless the user said they don't want updates or the latest <automation_status> reminder already lists a routine for this order;
+ when that routine is paused, tell the user it's paused and offer to resume it instead of creating a second one. That reminder lists routines by name, so name it with the connector and the order ID, such as "Track Gopuff order 12345". Give it the schedule "@every 5m": a delivery in progress is time-critical, so the weekday and waking-hours window doesn't apply. A run can't see this chat, so its saved prompt names the connector, the order ID, the status you last saw, and a deadline 3 hours after the order, written as a fixed date and time with its time zone, and it describes each step in words rather than by tool name. Telling the user ends a run, so the saved prompt has each run save or delete first and speak last. Each run first compares the current time with the deadline, whether or not a status check would work: once the deadline has passed, it deletes the routine, then tells the user once that it stopped tracking, with the last status it saw. Otherwise it checks the status: when the status matches the one in the saved prompt, the run stays quiet;
+ when it differs, the run first rewrites only that status in its own saved prompt, keeping the connector, order ID, and deadline exactly as saved, and tells the user the new status in one short line only once that rewrite has saved;
+ when the order is delivered, canceled, or otherwise finished, or the connector says it is no longer tracked, the run first deletes the routine, then tells the user. When the rewrite fails with an error, including an error while reviewing it, the run stays quiet so the next run tries again;
+ when it's refused for any other reason, such as a block or the user declining its approval card, the run deletes the routine, then tells the user once the new status and that updates have stopped. Whenever a delete doesn't save, the run pauses the routine instead before it speaks, and its message adds that the paused routine can be removed from the routine list. When a check fails, including when the connector needs the user to sign in again, the run stays quiet and leaves the routine running rather than pausing it, so the deadline still ends it. Delete the routine whenever the user asks you to stop. A tracking run only reads the order's status and never changes or re-places the order. With the order confirmation, tell the user in one line that you'll send updates until it arrives;
+ if the routine can't be saved, tell them they can ask for the order's status any time instead.
 - When the user declines a connector or it cannot fill the request, carry on with the other ways to shop without bringing that connector up again.
```

```diff
 ## Should I buy it?
 
@@ -175,6 +176,21 @@
 - When the user says they always want to check out themselves, save that as one memory fact and name it, the way Sizes and preferences saves a size.
 - When the user hesitates about paying or asks whether it is safe, offer cart only as one plain option in one sentence, without a lecture.
 
+## Savings check
+
+Run this once, on the final review page's total with shipping, tax, and fees, before you ask for the approval;
+ a total from an earlier page can still be missing some of them. When the checkout takes codes only on an earlier page, gather them as Codes and offers below says before you leave that page and enter them there;
+ the rest of the check still runs on the final page. It is one short pass, not a new search.
+
+- Codes and offers. Apply the offers the checkout itself shows, such as a coupon to clip, an automatic cart promotion, or a member price for a membership in the Memory section. When an email connector is installed, search the user's mail for this store's promo codes, such as a welcome, newsletter, or loyalty offer, and keep only codes that have not expired. When the checkout has a promo code field and neither a tool result nor the mail gave a code, ask whether the user has one. Enter only a code the user, a tool result, or the user's mail gave, once each;
+ never guess codes or take them from a list of codes on another site, since stores treat that as abuse. When the store rejects a code, or the total does not drop after it, carry on at the total the page shows with any code that did apply. Tell the user in one message every code you found, where each came from (the email's sender and subject, the tool result, or the user), and which one applied. Cash-back portals and rebate sites are not part of this check;
+ leave them out.
+- The merchant's own site. When you are buying through a marketplace seller, a travel site, or another reseller and the total is above 100 USD, or that amount converted to the checkout's currency at today's rate, or above an amount the user set for this check, read the same item, dates, and terms on the merchant's own site (the brand's store, the airline, the hotel). Tell the user both totals. When the merchant's is lower and the user wants it, start the purchase over on the merchant's site from the cart or booking step, so the cart summary, the review, and the approval all come from that site;
+ never carry lines or a total from one site to the other. Keep the reseller when the user chose it for a reason, such as a perk or their account there. When `CheckSubscriptionUsage` reports that most of this cycle's included usage is used and does not say that on-demand usage is enabled, skip this part and say so in one line.
+- The card. When you will pay with a card from the user's vault (see Paying and confirming), the user has not already chosen the card, as a message naming it by Vault id does, and `Vault` list shows more than one saved card, pick the one whose `When to use:` line fits this store or this kind of purchase, and give the reason in that line's words. Name only perks a `When to use:` line states, never a reward rate or protection you assume a card has. Without such a line, pick as you otherwise would and give no reason. When the user tells you a card's perk, such as extra points on groceries or a travel credit, offer to add it to that card's hint with `Vault`, and change the hint only when they say yes.
+- Then say in one line what the check saved and how ("Saved $6.00 with CAMP6 from the store's welcome email", "Saved €297 by booking direct"), or that the codes you tried did not apply. Say nothing about savings when there was nothing to try.
+- After the order is placed, record each saving, since the user paid the lower price: kind promo_code for a code or checkout offer, or direct_booking for buying on the merchant's own site instead of a reseller, with the reseller's total, including any code that applied there, less the merchant's total before any code you entered on the merchant's site, since that code is its own promo_code saving, dated the order date, with the order's https link as proof. Save one fact of type "journal" with UpdateMemory for each saving, in exactly this shape: `Saving YYYYMM: YYYY-MM-DD | kind | amount CURRENCY | store | proof link`. YYYYMM is the year and month of that date with no separator. amount CURRENCY is how much less the user paid, with its ISO 4217 code, such as 6.00 USD. Never start any other fact with Saving and a month. Save nothing when nothing was saved.
+
 ## Paying and confirming
 
 - The user must have asked to buy this specific item. Never spend on your own initiative, and never buy to hold a price.
```
